<meta>This page continues the author's critique of Oswald Croll’s "Preface of Wonders." Having previously established that Croll’s work is inherently magical rather than purely medical, the author now examines the specific psychological and "Gabalistic" (Kabbalistic) theories Croll uses to justify his claims about the power of the human mind and imagination.</meta>

# ON THE HORRIBLE IMPIETY OF PARACELSIAN MAGIC

### SECTION II.

<margin>Through imagination, the same things of the macrocosm <note>original: "macrocosm9"; the "Greater World" or the universe at large.</note> can be produced.</margin>
**W**hatever we see with our eyes in the greater world, says *Croll on page 38*, this same thing can also be produced by the imagination. Thus all herbs, all growing things, and all metals can be produced through the imagination and "True Gabalia" <note>A term Croll uses for his version of Kabbalah, the Jewish mystical tradition which Paracelsians adapted into a form of "natural magic."</note>, etc. Wondrous is the power of fantasy! This is a certain interior sensory faculty common to both man and beasts, which receives a perceived form from the common sense and converts it into a "phantasia" or image, which afterwards in man becomes the subject of the intellect. It fashions things in various ways and is stirred up in various ways. Often, when the internal material is pushed and repeated from memory, or even moved by a divine or other principle, it is agitated; hence it happens that it revolves not only the images of existing things, which it received from the external senses, but also of non-existing things, and of those things which are revealed in a hidden manner. Just as God says in *Numbers 12:6*: *If there be a prophet among you, I the Lord will appear to him in a vision, or I will speak to him in a dream, etc.* This faculty is miraculous in those afflicted by the diseases of mania, melancholy, or frenzy. Likewise in the suspicious, the fearful, those with a cauterized bad conscience, those in love, and similar people; just as in sleep it is moved in various ways through dreams, and often strikes terror with false images. It should be noted, however, that the word "phantasy" does not always mean only the internal sense which is called "imagining," but is often folded together with the thought in the "active and passive intellect" <note>Aristotelian terms for the parts of the mind that process information and store knowledge.</note>. It is not difficult to understand that, whatever we see outside with our eyes or perceive with other senses, the imagination creates an image of it within, since the same thing happens in animals according to nature. If we were to see all herbs, all growing things, and metals—or if we were to feel all worldly bodies, simple, mixed, celestial, and elementary, and all qualities, etc.—the imagination would generate the images of them all within. Truly, such power is not granted to man. For there is no sense for all things, whether in act or in potential. *The things we know are the smallest part of the things we do not know.* <note>original: "Ea quæ scimus sunt minima pars eorum quæ ignoramus"</note> And by what sense could you comprehend spiritual things, and those which belong to the pure intellect and fall under reason either in whole or in part? Therefore, if *Croll* were speaking physically, he would for the most part be pouring out empty words. But he proposes something magical and abstruse, which is to be effected through "True Gabalia." Therefore, although we have dealt with Paracelsian Gabalia in the preceding sections, we must still look into it, lest we be deceived by the exchange of the true for the false; we must also look into that "magical" or rather "Satanic" imagination, since it cannot be natural.

<margin>Natural imagination.</margin>
<margin>The force of imagination.</margin>
<margin>Croll's True Gabalia, and magical imagination.</margin>

---

### SECTION III.

<margin>Note the false columns of magic by which mages deceive themselves and others; the only true column is a conspiracy with Devils to do something according to one's will.</margin>
**T**rue Gabalia is a part of the so-called Kabbalistic Magic, he says, *which rests on three columns: first, true prayers made in spirit and truth, where the union of God and the created spirit occurs in the Holy of Holies, where God is addressed by the internal spirit, not by the force of words, but in sacred silence without the opening of the mouth or breath. Second, by natural faith or wisdom communicated and inborn by God the Father to all men EQUALLY as a common private property and inheritance. Third, by a strongly exalted imagination, whose great and wondrous power the Light of Nature clearly demonstrates, both in the rods of Jacob mentioned by Moses <note>A reference to Genesis 30:37–39, where Jacob uses peeled branches to influence the markings of lab animals' offspring.</note>, and in pregnant women who imprint the mark of a desired object upon the fetus, etc.* Heinrich Khunrath <note>A famous physician and hermetic philosopher (c. 1560–1605) known for his "Amphitheater of Eternal Wisdom."</note> previously published an "Amphitheater" which he called "Christian-Kabalistic and Divine-Magical," at the top of the first page of which the essential name of God, *Jehovah*, is fitted into a triangular schema, just as Serenus and other mages want the word *Abracadabra* to be written—the figure of a Pythagorean table or an inverted pyramid. From this you may recognize the agreement of the mages, derived from that Simon in the Acts of the Apostles, Menander, Basilides, and indeed from Zoroaster, Osthanes, Jannes and Jambres, Hermes, the Egyptians, and further back from the beginning of Babylon. Therefore, when *Croll* in his Gabalistic art joins and unites God (entreated by silent prayers) to phantasy and natural faith, and thinks that by the force of these joined things everything can be produced by a mage, he feels the same as the "Divine-Magical" Khunrath. From this we understand that when, in *Exodus 7*, Pharaoh called his wise men and sorcerers to change their rods into serpents, and by incantations to turn waters into blood and produce frogs, it must be judged that they accomplished this through *Croll's* Gabalistic art—unless perhaps the Paracelsians want even Aaron, Moses, the Prophets, Christ, and the Apostles to have performed their miracles by this same art of theirs.

<margin>The Amphitheater of Thrasybulus. <note>likely referring to a specific work or a pseudonym used in magical literature.</note></margin>
<margin>All mages can excuse their magic with the same arguments as Croll, even though they achieve everything by the help of the Devil.</margin>
For *Croll* produces everything through his Gabalistic art. When later, in *Numbers 22, 23, & 24*, Balaam wanted to stir up the Israelites by the help of Mages, he looked to this very science, and he had practiced it in many other things before, just as the sorcerers of the Pharaohs had, from which they had gained that fame and authority for themselves. For how else would kings have known that Mages could do such things? The same is to be thought of Simon Magus in *Acts 8:9-11*. The same of Elymas bar-Jesus, the mage and false prophet in *Acts 13:6-8*. Likewise of that Egyptian in *Acts 21:38* and in Josephus’ *Antiquities, book 20, chapter 14, and The Jewish War, book 2, chapter 12*. When jugglers present wonders to the eyes which seem to be, but are not, that is what it means for *Croll* to "produce through Divine-Magical Gabalistics." So it must be concluded concerning the "transmutations" of *Trithemius* <note>Johannes Trithemius (1462–1516), an abbot and famous scholar of cryptography and magic.</note>, concerning the magical dinners of Tiridates, concerning magical vineyards and grapes, and many other things. If you ask by what art the Thessalian Witches <note>original: "Thessalæ Sagæ"; ancient Greek witches famous for necromancy.</note> make the dead stand up—as is also told of Trithemius—and by what art *Samuel* was brought back to life in *1 Kings 28* <note>The story of the Witch of Endor.</note>, you have the answer from *Croll*. Gabalistics can produce everything, whatever is visible in the world. Even *Roger Bacon* <note>A 13th-century English philosopher often associated (sometimes falsely) with early science and magic.</note> knew that "representative art," but he did not think there was a need for magical impiety for it, since it can be performed through mirrors—unless he deceived himself in his "Wonders." Rejoice, O mages, and boldly practice your art! You have *Croll* as a patron, protected also by public privilege. You have the whole *Paracelsian* system, and the "Divine-Magical Amphitheater of Thrasybulus," which *Croll* judged worthy of cedar oil <note>Meaning "worthy of preservation."</note>, but which we commit to the heretical fires. It is a wonder, however, if this art is true, why everything was not permitted to the Pharaohic and Babylonian mages, to Simon, Elymas, Balaam, and the rest. For *Croll* cannot say that they did anything against God, because that Gabalistic art makes God and the "Gabalian" man one. But neither can he affirm that their Gabalia was false and his own true, because he himself never produced these things which those mages did.

<margin>Gabalistics, or "the art of thieves" <note>original: "die Gabelsunsi/ars furcariū"; likely a pun on Gabel (fork/gallows) and the art of those who use "witches' forks" or gallows-birds.</note> which witches use. See the colloquy of the 3 volumes, the second of the dog-days of Maiolus.</margin>
<margin>If all things can be produced through Gabalistics, there would have been some Gabalist who produced everything by his art: first spiritually, then corporally. But there was none.</margin>

<summary>This page critiques the Paracelsian concept of "Gabalistics" and the "exalted imagination," arguing that Croll's theories are merely a rebranding of ancient, forbidden sorcery. The author links these ideas to historical and biblical mages, asserting that such "divine magic" is actually a deceptive conspiracy with demonic forces.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the author's polemic against Oswald Croll’s "Preface of Wonders." The previous page discussed the power of imagination; here, the author transitions into a philological and theological critique of the term "Magic" and the specific impieties he perceives in Croll’s "Gabalistic" (Kabbalistic) system.</meta>

...according to Croll's opinion.
<sig>F 2</sig>
<meta>catchword: turn (tur)</meta>

**T**he Magi were held as wise men by the whole world at that time, as is clear from *Genesis 41:8*: *Chacamim* <note>original: "Chacamim"; Hebrew for 'wise men.'</note>, who in *Daniel 2:2* are called *Soothsayers, Magi, Sorcerers, and Chaldeans*, and in verse 12, *The Wise Men of Babylon*, as also in verses 13, 24, and 27: *Chartymmim, Asschaphim, Mecaschphim, Casdim, Chakkimei Babel* <note>These are various Hebrew and Aramaic terms for diviners, enchanters, sorcerers, and Chaldean sages.</note>. A *Chartam* <note>original: "Chartymmim"</note> is a magician and a caster of horoscopes, whom they also call a physicist and a wise man of nature. *Aschaph* signifies a philosopher and an astronomer. *Schindler* <note>Valentin Schindler, a noted 16th-century Hebraist.</note> cites Strabo, who also calls them Chaldean philosophers. These same men are also physicians. *Mecaschph* is a deceiver or a soothsayer, a kind which God forbade to live: *Leviticus 19:26, chapter 20:6, 27; Deuteronomy 18:10, 11, 12; 1 Kings 28:9; 2 Kings 23:24; Isaiah 19:3, 11, 12, 13; chapter 44:25*. Such were Jannes and Jambres <note>The traditional names of the Egyptian magicians who opposed Moses.</note> against Moses: *2 Timothy 3:8*. The root is *Caschaph*, meaning to practice magic, sorcery, and deceptions. The *Cassedim* are the Chaldeans, who professed mathematics, astrology, and magic. *Chacam* is a wise, learned man, a craftsman, a philosopher, a professor of arts, a scholar, and a physician whom *Avicenna* calls *Galen*. *Chakamim* are such wise men, from the root *Chakam*, meaning he was wise, he knew, etc. From these things it is plain that those who were held as magi by the ancients were also, for the most part (according to common opinion), skilled in the *Paracelsian Gabalia* <note>The author uses "Gabalia" to refer to the Paracelsian adaptation of the Kabbalah, a Jewish mystical tradition.</note> of that time. Therefore, even the Greeks, who learned from the Chaldeans, Egyptians, and Gymnosophists <note>Ancient Indian philosophers known for their asceticism and "nakedness."</note>, were not unskilled in magic, such as: *Socrates, Plato, Apollonius, Manethon, Democritus, Pythagoras, etc.* But since not all things <margin>*Paracelsian magicians are in the greatest abomination before God.*</margin> were possible for them to produce, it must be judged that *Croll* and similar Paracelsians either lie most impudently (as the reality itself speaks, for they cannot do what they profess), or they are skilled in an art of an excellent degree; but *the more excellent it is, the more abominable it is before God*. Moses was learned in all the wisdom of the Egyptians (*Acts 7:22*), but without doubt he judged it vain and abominated it. He studied magic as one devoted to wisdom, but not the magic forbidden in the Law. For he is never read to have practiced those magical studies; and if he had thought them true, he would have proposed some foundations in his books from which Croll’s principles could be deduced. Likewise, magic is attributed to *Solomon*. But here too, he has nothing similar to the Crollians. Let one read the first chapter of *Proverbs*, also chapters 8 and 9, along with chapters 7, 8, and 9 of the *Book of Wisdom*, which is ascribed to him. Neither the magic of *Paracelsus*, nor *Croll*, nor *Hartmann*, nor others will be found in them. Thus it is a wonder that the Paracelsian magicians count Solomon among their own, since they cannot show even a single principle of their art from the books of Solomon. Some say certain magical books were attributed to him, but that King *Hezekiah* burned them along with the bronze serpent; and a certain axiom of his is cited: *In herbs, words, and stones, there is great virtue.* It may be that he who did not abstain from foreign women and their rites also left a place for magic; but just as *Cornelius Agrippa* <note>A famous Renaissance occultist who later wrote a "Recantation" of the vanity of the sciences and magic.</note> detested the vanity of his own magic, so too it seems did *Solomon*, who set his mind to seek and investigate wisely concerning all things done under the sun: and he saw all things, and behold, all is vanity and affliction of spirit. *All things are full of labor; man cannot explain them in speech.* The Devil transferred that pride from *Eve* and himself into the *Paracelsians*.

<margin>*Ecclesiastes 1:8, 14. Solomon seemed to find magical things, but found them vain, as did Nero. Thus magical books were perhaps found in his possession, but he was not the author.*</margin>

---

-> S E C T I O  IV. <-

<margin>*The horrendous impiety of Croll by which he makes God and the devil [one], and joins truth with a lie.*</margin>
<margin>*John 16:13; John 4:23, 24; John 14:6.*</margin>
**T**here is no more execrable impiety than to so licentiously, in the midst of the assembly of Christian people, as if with a certain boasting and presumption of abstruse wisdom, horribly *profane the name of God*, and through diabolical persuasion make *Gabalistic Magic the first pillar of Paracelsus*. They claim *true prayers made to God in spirit and truth* are where a union of God and *that magical spirit which he calls Olympic and Starry* <note>original: "Olympicum, sydereumq;"</note> occurs in the Holy of Holies. True prayers to God are nothing other than the worship due to God alone, by which we acknowledge God’s omnipotence, omniscience, and omnipresence, and ask from Him, as the benevolent *author of all excellent gifts*, for spiritual and temporal things—not only in spirit and truth, but also according to His promise and will (*John 4:23*). In that verse, by *spirit* is not understood *Croll’s Magic Spirit*, which he calls the internal man, but the *Holy Spirit*; and by *truth* is understood both the name of the Son of God (who commands us to pray in his name and calls himself the way, the life, and the truth) and the true Word and Spirit of God, which are themselves called truth (*1 John 3:22*): *Whatever we ask, we shall receive from him, because we keep his commandments and do those things that are pleasing in his sight.* And chapter 5, verse 14: *This is the confidence (boldness)* <note>The Greek word "parrhesia" (παρρησία) is noted for its sense of "boldness" or "frankness."</note> *that we have toward God, that if we ask anything according to his will, he hears us.* From these things we argue against Paracelsian impiety thus: 1. *If that Spirit in which God is to be adored is the Holy Spirit, God Himself, who helps our infirmity and intercedes for us* (*Romans 8:26, 27*), *it is not that Crollian magical spirit.* For the Spirit of God cannot be that fictitious spirit of the magicians as described by Croll. This is certain: as proved by the Apostle, and the sacred interpreters of *John 4* understand the same, who also (with Christ as witness) suggests all sayings to the pious, including this: that the Father must be invoked in the name of the Son. And therefore, consequently, whoever twists the Word of God into a sense foreign to its genuine meaning profanes the name of God and is a blasphemer against the Holy Spirit. 2. *If God is to be adored in the true Word of God, the name of Christ, and the Holy Spirit, Gabalistic magic does not have true prayers as its pillar.* For as all things are produced by you which are in the macrocosm <note>the universe</note> through Gabalistic magic, it is not prayer in the Holy Spirit, the name of Christ, and the true Word. Let it be proven by even a single saying of Scripture where it is clear that God wants us to ask for such a "production" from Him, or that we should invoke a helper in Gabalistic magic—a term unknown to the canonical letters. The Devil’s promise is: *All these things I will give to you, etc.* (*Matthew 4:9*). Where would be the cross of the Church if those vows were valid before God? 3. *Those things asked in true prayers to God in the Holy Spirit have a promise of being heard, because the adoration is done according to God’s will.* But the effects of Gabalistic magic have no promise of being heard, because it is commanded by no will of God. 4. The Lord is tempted by those prayers. 5. That "production" is denied to human powers; and since either spiritual goods are to be asked from God, or temporal goods that are lawful, possible, and honest, looking toward the honor of God and the benefit of one's neighbor, these magical things are contrary to all of them. For I ask, what is the magical production of all things seen with eyes in the macrocosm other than a rash persuasion, a part of diabolical arrogance, a tempting of the divine name, and a desire for a certain divinity and equality with God? It is called a union...

<margin>*John 16:13, 14; chapter 14:26.*</margin>

<summary>The author critiques the terminology of magic using biblical Hebrew and Greek philology, arguing that Paracelsian "Gabalistic" magic is a diabolical perversion of true Christian prayer and the Holy Spirit. He asserts that biblical figures like Moses and Solomon rejected forbidden magic, contrary to the claims of Oswald Croll.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the author's polemic against the Paracelsian school, specifically targeting Oswald Croll and his claims about the "Gabalistic" (Kabbalistic) power of the magician. The previous page ended with a discussion on the various Hebrew terms for magicians and wise men; here, the author argues that the magician’s desire to mimic God's creative power is a form of Luciferian pride.</meta>

# On the Horrible Impiety of Paracelsian Magic

...[is united] with God. And the magus <note>A practitioner of magic.</note> asks that he might be able to produce all things that God does: to make heaven and earth out of nothing, to create light and the sun with the entire firmament, trees, cattle, humans, birds, fish, gold, gems; and what can he not achieve? For all these things are seen in the greater world <note>The "macrocosm" or the universe at large.</note>. Let a pious mind consider whether this is not that very boast of Lucifer in *Isaiah 14:12-13*: *I will ascend into heaven, I will exalt my throne above the stars of God* (Recognize here Croll’s exalted imagination) *I will sit on the mount of the assembly, in the sides of the North* (spread over nothingness or the void, *Job 26:7*). *I will ascend above the heights of the clouds* (I shall be united with God, I will produce all things just as He does) *I will be like the Most High*? Add to this that true wisdom and perfect good must be sought from God. Gabalistic Magic <note>A term used by these authors to refer to a Christianized, often magical interpretation of the Jewish Kabbalah.</note> is foolishness, mere deception, and diabolical malice. Therefore, it cannot be sought from God. 

<margin>
Nothing is more execrable than Paracelsian magic.
The Paracelsists are sacrilegious.
</margin>

This assumption is proven by comparison with the acts of the most famous magicians and witches. Furthermore, if this were something divine that God granted to those who ask, why can these magicians do nothing without the devil? Since Paracelsian Magic professes these abominable and cursed things, is there still anyone who dares to profess himself a Christian and yet both teach and defend this magic, and release it into the public with official privilege? Is there anyone who glories in being a Paracelsian? Can any Church that wishes to appear Christian tolerate such a great scandal? Why are such books not burned, like those of Solomon and the Ephesians in *Acts 19*? Is it a greater thing to remove bodies from our midst than to destroy souls with the diabolical Magic of Paracelsus? They prohibit the magical works of *Cornelius Agrippa* <note>Heinrich Cornelius Agrippa (1486–1535), a famous polymath and occult writer.</note>, yet they circulate *Croll’s* works with privileges, even though these are more cursed than those. It is a sacrilege to transfer the true prayers of the pious to the impious Gabalistic magic of the Paracelsians. Therefore, that "first column" of *Croll's* proceeded from Satan, the father of lies, and not from any good spirit.

---

-> S E C T I O N V. <-

<margin>
The union of God with the Gabalistic spirit.
Deut. 16:6.
2 Chron. 6:18.
Psal. 22:4.
Sirach 24:13.
The magical Holy of Holies.
Psal. 113:5.
Ps. 75:6. "Lift not up your horn on high."
Ps. 81:8.
Ps. 91:14-15.
Ps. 50:15.
Livy, Book 1, concerning Accius Navius the augur, who carried out the thought of Tarquin.
</margin>

WHERE the union of God and the created Spirit may be in the Holy of Holies <note>original: "sancto sanctorum"</note>, says the magus. But the Holy of Holies in which God dwells is first the temple of eternity, *Isaiah 57:15, 1 Timothy 6:16*: God alone has immortality and dwells in inaccessible light, whom no man has seen nor can see. *Psalm 123:1*: God dwells in the heavens, *Psalm 2:4*. Wisdom is in the highest: *Sirach 24:7, John 14:23*. The Holy Trinity comes to those who keep the word of God and makes a dwelling with them. *Ephesians 3:17*: Christ dwells through faith in the hearts of the pious. Their body is the temple of the Holy Spirit who is in them. *1 Cor. 6:19, ch. 3:16, 2 Cor. 6:16*. He also dwells in the public Church, which was prefigured by the Holy of Holies of Solomon’s temple, from where He gave answers, just as today He does from the Holy Bible. *Psalm 132:13*: *The Lord has chosen Zion for his habitation. This is my rest for ever and ever: here will I dwell, for I have chosen it.* *1 Kings 6:12, 13, 19; ch. 8:12, 13, 27*. Christ promises that He will be in the midst of two or three gathered in His name, *Matthew 18:20*. He is also said to be and to stand in the congregation of the Gods, or judges, *Psalm 82*. *Wherever God dwells, He dwells in the holy place.* *Psalm 22:3*, etc. 

It is not without merit that one asks what that "Holy of Holies" is, into which the prayers of the Gabalistic magus penetrate. Sacred scripture recognizes none: the prayers of the pious ascend and pierce the clouds to heaven and reach the ears of God; but concerning the prayer of the Gabalist, there is not even a little word in the sacred writings, nor can there be, since it is impious and dares to demand from God the production of all those things seen in the world. *Who is like the Lord our God, who dwells on high, and looks down upon the humble things in heaven and on earth?* There is only one Michael, Jesus Christ the Son of God <note>The author identifies the Archangel Michael with Christ, a theological position held by some reformers.</note>. All others cannot even be compared with God, as is found in Job both elsewhere and in *chapters 38, 39*, etc., where God addresses Job himself on this matter. 

But concerning magicians, what is written you may find in *Isaiah 47:10* and following. From what has been said, the argument is: *If there is nowhere any Holy of Holies into which the prayers of the Gabalistic magus can enter, then what Croll writes about these prayers is false. The first is true: therefore also the second.* The assumption is proven because it would have to be the immortality, eternity, and light of God—but this is inaccessible to every creature—or it would have to be the Church of God, the Word of God, the heart of the faithful, or the tribunal of justice. But it is none of these. Magical arts are prohibited in the Church and the Word of God; in the hearts of the faithful there is nothing of magic; in the tribunal of justice, the laws are opposed to it. 

You might say: *God fulfills the desire of those who fear Him, and is near to all who call upon Him in truth* (*Psalm 145:18-19*). I answer: The Psalm speaks of those who fear God, and not the curious who affect divinity through magic and desire to produce all the visible things God produced. Secondly, it speaks of the truth which is Christ, and His true word, and the name of Christ, which contains not those things which have not been commanded of us, but those things which pertain to the duty of those who fear God. Thirdly, since the Psalm carries a promise of salvation, it discusses this invocation which belongs to the afflicted who need salvation. But the Magus dreams for himself all blessed things and productions according to the power of God for the display of his own strengths. Here, therefore, in invoking God for his own purpose, he has no protection from the promise of the Psalm. For he seeks things higher than himself, and scrutinizes majesty, nor is he wise according to duty, but is occupied with sublime matters beyond what he ought. He does not "do his own business" <note>original Greek: "τὰ ἑαυτῶ" (ta heautou)</note>, but is a "busybody" <note>original Greek: "πολυπραγμων" (polypragmōn) - one who meddles in many things.</note> against the rules and principles of Christian piety (*Romans 12:16, Galatians 6:3, 1 Peter 4:15*). 

Furthermore, since this Gabalistic magic is the same in its foundations as that of the Egyptian Hermes, Cornelius Agrippa, Trithemius, the Chaldeans, Tiridates, Basilides, Menander, Simon, Elymas, and the like, who likewise studied to produce all things—as did Jannes and Mambres <note>The Egyptian magicians who opposed Moses.</note>, and the augur Navius, who thought he could fulfill his own thoughts—it appears that if Croll's prayers were just and penetrated into the Holy of Holies of God, those of the others would have been and penetrated as well. But this is not the case. Therefore, neither does Johannes Hartmann <note>Johannes Hartmann (1568–1631), a chemist and professor of "Chymiatria" (chemical medicine).</note> propose any other Holy of Holies in his "Vital Philosophy"; because it is a fantastic fiction, it neither possesses God nor admits any other.

-> S E C T I O N VI. <-

THE union of God and the created Spirit in the Holy of Holies. It is extreme impiety to mix God with magical rites in such a way that He becomes one with the cursed and abominable spirit of the Magus. I should not think [anyone] so in-

<summary>This page critiques the Paracelsian concept of "Gabalistic Magic," arguing that the magician’s attempt to mimic God’s creative power is a form of Luciferian pride and that God's "Holy of Holies" is inaccessible to such diabolical arts.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the author’s refutation of Oswald Croll’s "Gabalistic" magic. The author is arguing that the magician's claim to a "union" with God is a blasphemous imitation of the unique relationship between Christ and the Father, or the spiritual union of the believer.</meta>

header: according to Croll's opinion.
sig: F 3
<meta>catchword: produce-</meta>

...to define the Magus *Croll* <note>Oswald Croll (c. 1563–1609), a prominent follower of Paracelsus.</note> as wishing for an "essential union" to take place, even though he was not ashamed to write that *the soul breathed into Adam’s face is nothing other than God Himself* <note>original: "animam inspiratam in faciem Adami nihil aliud esse quam Deum ipsum"</note>, and to make this soul eternal. As if anything else could be eternal, or be God in essence, other than the most holy Trinity, the creator of all things! It is well established that the Trinity is altogether blessed and uncreated, while the human soul is created, and thus subject to divine omnipotence; so much so that, along with its body, it can be cast into eternal fire for torments and torture.

Regarding the "union of the will," I believe he intended that from the combined will of both God and the *magus*, there should follow the operation and production of all things that are seen produced in the great world <note>The macrocosm or universe.</note>. But since the will of God precedes all, just as we pray: *Thy will be done* <note>original: "Fiat voluntas tua"</note>; and as Christ said in His Agony: *Not what I will, or as I will, but as You will* <note>Matthew 26:39; original: "Non quod ego, vel sicut ego, sed sicut tu vis"</note>. It is necessary that we desire nothing except *what has been revealed as God’s will*. Otherwise, we do not ask according to God’s will. Since no revelation has ever been made regarding a *will for the production of Gabalistic <note>A term for the "Christian Kabbalah" practiced by Paracelsians.</note> magic*, but rather a prohibition and severe interdict stands in more than one place in the Word of God, it follows that this "magical union" is a diabolical invention. For there is no middle ground between Christ and Belial <note>A name for the devil.</note>. *If a union is not made with Christ, who is God blessed forever, through whom the Father created the world, it must necessarily be made with Belial*, unless the whole thing is a fiction and the most vain delusion of a man with a deceived mind.

And yet they do not want it to be a delusion; for already the Egyptian Magicians produced something, and the stories of the banquets, vineyards, and other magical transformations of *Trithemius* <note>Johannes Trithemius (1462–1516), a Benedictine abbot and famous occultist.</note> in the presence of *Pico* <note>Giovanni Pico della Mirandola (1463–1494), the famous Renaissance philosopher.</note> are well known, as *Croll* writes. If, therefore, a union of two spirits occurs, because one is not good and divine, it must be a union of evil with evil, and thus a Satanic delusion as much as a conspiracy. For what Satan himself does, he persuades the *Magus* is being done by them, just as he deceives wretched witches with wondrous transmutations, the summoning of storms, and other things.

How well it would have gone for Adam if he had known of this "union"! It would have been said in vain, "Cursed is the ground because of you," and the rest; it would have been said in vain to Cain, the father of Magicians: "When you till the ground," etc. (Genesis 4:13). The threats of God in Deuteronomy 28 would be void if any carnal man could do what Croll wants him to be able to do—though Croll himself could do nothing in this matter except lie. A pious soul considers: if there were even one man who could do all the things he sees, how much would be taken away from the glory of the Most High God, who alone does all that He wills? 

What kind of "Holy of Holies" is it in which the devil and the *Gabalistic spirit of the magus meet*? This is not always an enthusiastic rapture to places outside the man—nor is Croll speaking of that—but rather a secret meeting, a workshop, or rather a sewer of unclean spirits within the Magus himself must be understood. That wretched man thinks his spirit goes out from him into the height of heaven; but he does not notice the devil already dwelling within him, just as he entered into Judas the traitor (John 13:27) and into Saul (1 Samuel 16:14). And such was the union between Simon Magus, or *Elymas*, and *Satan*. The heart of the magus is that most impure *Gabalistic Holy of Holies*. For Satan rejoices in such a dwelling (Matthew 12:43-44). We shall speak of Hartmann’s "Holy of Holies" in its proper place. For in that, we shall meet in our own way, not in Croll's magical way.

---

### SECTION VII.

<margin>2 Corinthians 6:17.</margin>
**B**ut regarding the union of God and men, let us say a bit more. In our first state of integrity, we were sons of God. Thus, we were also one with God in love, justice, holiness, and the perfect image. Just as children are one with parents, so were we with God. From this living unity we fell through death resulting from sin, and from friends we were made enemies; from children of love, children of wrath and slaves of Satan, who held us shut up in his prisons for punishment. In this alienation, what union could there be? The vision of God, which the pious will enjoy, is lost forever [in this state]. There is no union here.

*Jesus Christ, the Son of God, by assuming human flesh, reconciled us to God the Father*. Here, man was first united to God through Him, by the personal union of Christ's humanity with the God who is the creator of all; such a union is denied to all others. We enjoy that union through the grace of redemption, of restored justice, of the faith of holiness, and the other things which Christ recovered for us, until the restoration begun in this life is perfected in the next. There, the image of God—lost indeed by our sin, but fully restored in us by the justice of Christ—will shine forth, and moreover will be decorated with unspeakable glory. 

The tightest bond of union is the love of the Father toward the Son, in whom He so loved the world that He gave Him for its life. He, in His assumed flesh, sits at the right hand of God, protects us, and intercedes for us. Next is the *knot of constant faith in Christ*, through which God dwells in us and we please God. *Whatever is not of faith is sin. It is impossible to please God without faith* (Hebrews 11:6). For it is necessary that he who approaches God must believe. *What? That He is, and that He is a rewarder of those who seek Him*; or as Christ explains in John, it is necessary to love God and keep His commandments. *This is eternal life, that they might know You, the only true God, and Jesus Christ whom You have sent* (John 17:3). *For there is no other name in which there is salvation* (Acts 4:12). *He who clings to the Lord is one spirit* <note>1 Corinthians 6:17.</note>. For true trust embraces God dwelling in us through faith (Ephesians 3:17). We are made **partakers of the divine nature** <note>original Greek: "tēs theias koinōnoi physeōs" (2 Peter 1:4).</note>, says Peter, through the precious promises in the knowledge of God and of Jesus our Lord, by which He has given us all things of His divine power for life and godliness through the knowledge of Him who called us by glory and virtue, so that we might flee the corruption of worldly lust. 

*Now indeed we are the sons of God*, says John (1 Epistle, chapter 3:2), *but it has not yet appeared what we shall be. But we know that when He appears, we shall be like Him, because we shall see Him as He is. And everyone who has this hope sanctifies himself just as He is holy*. When the Felician heresy <note>A reference to Adoptianism, associated with Felix of Urgel in the 8th century.</note> interpreted that "likeness" as mere adoption, the Council of Frankfurt <note>A church council held in 794 AD that condemned Adoptianism.</note> referred it to the inner man, who is renewed in the recognition of God and in charity until the image is perfectly restored (Colossians 3:10; 2 Corinthians 4:10, 16). God will be all in all (1 Corinthians 15:28). From these sayings, a pious and Christian man gathers these arguments:

<margin>The Gabalistic production is non-existent.</margin>
1. Before the Fall, in the uncorrupted image of God in man, there was no such union with God that tended toward the "production" of the visible works of the world. For God the Creator alone refuses to give His glory to another. Moreover, the restoration to that image through Christ, which is completed in the time of refreshment and restoration of all things, is at least begun in this life. Therefore, there is not, nor will there be, any such union by which [a man] produces...

<summary>The author continues his attack on Oswald Croll, arguing that the "magical union" claimed by Paracelsians is actually a diabolical possession rather than a divine gift. He contrasts this with the orthodox Christian understanding of union with God through Christ, faith, and the restoration of the imago Dei.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the author’s systematic refutation of the "magical union" proposed by Oswald Croll and Heinrich Khunrath. The author is arguing that the "Gabalistic" (Kabalist) claim that a human can be essentially united with God to perform miracles is both a theological impossibility and a pagan deception.</meta>

# On the Horrible Impiety of Paracelsian Magic

...man might produce the visible works of the world; and consequently, the Gabalistic <note>A term for "Christian Kabbalah," a mystical practice involving the use of Hebrew names and divine powers, popular among Paracelsian philosophers.</note> theory of the Magi is a most vain invention, and blasphemous toward the glory of God. It is clear that if there will be no such union in the next life, it is much less possible to exist in this life of sinners.

2. If any such union existed, it would be in Christ, in whom we are made complete, and in whom the whole fullness of divinity dwells bodily. (Colossians 2:9). But it is not in Christ. For it would be included in the faith of Christ, which has been preached in the world. Yet the faith of Christ contains no such thing; indeed, the principles of the Old and New Testaments oppose it, and warn us to beware of that philosophy which tries to lead us away to another foundation.

3. If our sins divide us from our God, no one in whom there is sin can be perfectly united to God, but it is necessary that the body of sin be destroyed, and we be reconciled to God through Christ. The Gabalistic Magi [Kabalistic magicians] sin excessively in this very thing, because they convince themselves of that fictional union, and teach others with public scandal, and thus depart furthest from God. How then will they be united to God to produce any works similar to those of the creators?

4. He who knows neither God, nor the way to God, nor has faith through which he may be reconciled to God, cannot be united to God. The Gabalistic spirit of the Magi does not know God: because it does not know the Son of God, and he who does not know him, does not know the Father. (Matthew 11:27). Thus, he who does not remain in the teaching of Christ has neither the Father nor the Son. (2 John verse 9). Moreover, it is obvious that he does not know the Son of God, because all that magic is pagan, and does not belong to the Church; it has its origin and its precepts from the Chaldean and Egyptian Mathematicians <note>In this period, "Mathematicians" usually referred to astrologers and casters of horoscopes.</note>, Soothsayers, etc. Hence both Croll and Khunrath commend the teaching of Hermes <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary Hellenistic figure to whom many foundational magical and alchemical texts were attributed.</note>. He [the Magus] is also ignorant of the way to God, which is Christ, in whom the Father is to be seen. (John 14:6, 7, 9). He does not have the faith through which the door to the dwelling of God—which is Christ—is grasped. He boasts indeed of some "natural faith," but this is not that faith by which Christ is apprehended and access to God is granted. If anyone wishes to be united to God, he must walk through the way to God and the door. That is nothing other than Christ and the Spirit of Christ. Therefore, it is not Croll’s Gabalistic spirit. It follows, then, that the Gabalistic spirit cannot be united to God.

<margin>John 14:7.</margin>

5. He is not united to God essentially, nor personally, nor through the perfection of an image, nor through faith, nor through the gifts of God—holiness, perfection, justice, mercy, and others which the Holy Spirit works in the hearts of the faithful and begins in this life, so that at least we are not found naked, but through imperfection we strive toward perfection, such as love for both God and neighbor, and the keeping of God's commandments. With what face [temerity], then, can they say, *the Magi's Gabalistic spirit is united to God?* If they think the premise is false, let them demonstrate where there is a promise of divine operation, and the operation itself. St. Peter lists various works of the Holy Spirit; Paul also lists them among these, yet no "magical union" with God is read. (1 Corinthians chapter 12, verse 4, and following).

6. The promises concerning access to the true God and true union are universal and common to all the faithful. That magical union, however, is not only not common, but is also a figment and a deception of foolish opinion, *by which the Magi think they are united to God, when they are actually united to the devil.* How then can it be a true union?

7. Worldly wisdom is from pagan frauds and errors, and it stands out in the writings of profane men, who, although they say they are friends of God and certain "Gods," or even [performers of] great miracles, in reality they are furthest from God. St. Paul calls such wisdom *folly*, through which the world or any prince of the age—whether he be "Thrice-Greatest" [Trismegistus] once, twice, or three times—cannot know God.

<margin>1 Cor. 1:20, 21; ch. 2:8, 9, 10.</margin>

8. It is a wonder that these impious Magi promise themselves such a union in this life, when the whole of life is a state of imperfection. (1 Corinthians 13:9). He who sins does not see God, nor has he known Him. (1 John 3:6). And who would deny that the Magi sin while they teach this very thing?

9. Even if such a union between the created spirit of man and God could exist in this life as the Magi propose, it would have occurred between God and one of the Saints of the Old or New Testament. But not even one example is read. And the Saints opposed such Magi and cursed them, as is read of Simon Magus, who by his incantations had brought it about *that he was called the great power of God* <note>original: "vt magna Dei virtus diceretur"; see Acts 8:10</note>, which neither Paracelsus, nor Croll, nor Khunrath, nor Agrippa, Trithemius, Artephius, Techellus, and the like, have achieved. To more fully understand what that "magical union" means, let the words of Trismegistus from the *Poimandres* <note>The first tractate of the *Corpus Hermeticum*, describing a vision of the creation of the universe.</note> be repeated, which Khunrath inscribed under the Chymical Table of Hermes in his *Amphitheater*: "When I was thinking about the nature of things," (says Hermes) "and raised the edge of my mind to higher things, the senses of the body being now lulled to sleep, just as usually happens to those who are heavy with sleep due to satiety or exhaustion" (more correctly, those who suffer ecstasy and trances such as those witches experience when they believe they are being carried away after anointing themselves, yet they only collapse as if dead, except that they jerk as if they were riding [horses]) <note>The author is comparing the "divine" visions of the magicians to the pharmacological hallucinations or demonic "flights" attributed to witches.</note> calling out in this manner: "What is it, O Mercury [Hermes], that you desire both to hear and to behold? What is it that you wish to learn and understand?" Then I said, "Who are you?" "I am," he said, "Pimander, *the mind of divine power*, and see what you want. I myself will be with you everywhere." "I desire," I said, "to learn the nature of things and to know God." To this he replied: "Embrace me in your mind, and I will instruct you in all things that you wish." When he had said these things, he changed his form, and suddenly revealed all things. So far from Hermes. Khunrath adds: *Thus also may the doctrine be to the son of faith: Amen.* We who are Christians do not say so: but with John, *Test the spirits to see if they are from God:* (1 John 4:1) for Satan [transforms himself into] an angel of light (2 Corinthians 11:14). Therefore we undertake an examination. In the times of the Apostles, those Hermetic things were already in the world among the nations, and flourished in the schools of the Magi and Platonists. But what does Peter say? *We did not follow cleverly devised myths when we made known to you the power and presence of our Lord Jesus Christ, but we were eyewitnesses of his majesty; we heard that voice brought from heaven;*

<margin>The reputation of Simon Magus was far greater than that of any other Gabalista [Kabalist].

The magical spirit of the Gabalista, and the Hermetic God, between whom the magical union must occur.

Satan as an angel of light.</margin>

<margin>1 John 4:1.
2 Cor. 11:14.</margin>

<summary>The author systematically rejects the possibility of a "magical union" with God as claimed by Paracelsian Kabbalists, arguing that such a union is reserved for Christ and that the magicians' visions are actually pagan deceptions or demonic hallucinations akin to those of witches.</summary>
<keywords>Paracelsian magic, Oswald Croll, Heinrich Khunrath, Kabbalah, Hermes Trismegistus, Poimandres, Simon Magus, spiritual union, demonic deception, heresy</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the author’s critique of the "magical union" described by Oswald Croll and Heinrich Khunrath. Having argued that true union with God is only found through Christ and faith, the author now specifically attacks the Hermetic "revelations" that these thinkers use to justify their alchemical and magical practices.</meta>

-># According to Croll’s opinion.<-
->F 4<-

...having been brought forward: *And we have a more sure word of prophecy, to which you do well to take heed* <note>original: "Et habemus firmiorem propheticum sermonem cui attendentes bene facitis"; 2 Peter 1:19.</note>, etc. Likewise John: *That which was from the beginning, which we have heard, which we have seen with our eyes and our hands have handled, of the Word of life we announce to you* <note>1 John 1:1.</note>. Likewise Abraham in Luke 16:29: *they have Moses and the Prophets*, etc. Likewise Christ: *Search the scriptures* <note>John 5:39.</note>, etc. Add to these the commands of God in Deuteronomy 18:15, Acts 3:22, Acts 7:37, and Matthew 17:5. Since, therefore, <margin>1 John 1:1. John 5:39.</margin> **Croll**, **Khunrath**, **Hartmann**, and other **Paracelsians** <note>Followers of Paracelsus (1493–1541), who blended medicine, alchemy, and mysticism.</note> wish to be called Christians, what stands in their way so that they do not listen to Christ and his disciples, but rather to **Hermes** <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary Hellenistic figure to whom the "Hermetic" writings on magic and alchemy are attributed.</note>, a pagan magus, in this super-celestial cause regarding union with God for the performing of miracles? Or is that passage of Paul in 2 Corinthians 4:3 <note>The text says 2 Cor 3:3, but the quote is 4:3.</note> applicable: *If our Gospel be hid, it is hid to them that are lost?* Likewise 2 Thessalonians 2:10: *Because they received not the love of the truth, that they might be saved, for this cause God shall send them strong delusion, that they should believe a lie?* The entire pagan philosophy of the ancients believed Hermes and strenuously opposed the doctrine of the Church. But what does God say from heaven? *In this my beloved son I am well pleased: hear him.* "Repent," cries the Son, "and believe (not Hermes, but) the Gospel." We also read the books of the Heathen, but we apply judgment based on divine principles. In the question of union with God for performing these miracles, the pagan disciplines are not even to be listened to. Tell me then, you **Hartmann**, you black Philosopher <note>Johannes Hartmann (1568–1631), the first professor of "Chymiatria." The "black" likely refers to the "dark" arts of alchemy or a pun on his name.</note> who loves magical intelligences in place of these things: have your magi not deserted Christ and betaken themselves to the devil? You too, **Scheunemann**, must acknowledge this along with **Gramanus**, you who wish to be *Paracelsists* and who, in the explanation of words, substitute the magical portents of Paracelsus. But let us examine that *Pimandric* Spirit <note>Referring to "Poimandres," the first book of the Corpus Hermeticum, where a divine "Mind" or "Shepherd" appears to Hermes in a vision.</note> brought back onto the stage by Croll and Khunrath.

**Firstly**, we know of certain revelations in the Sacred Scriptures by which God manifested himself to the Fathers, Prophets, and Apostles, whether by visible appearance, by voice, or other modes. For He appeared to the first-created in paradise, and after; He spoke to Cain, and doubtless also to Enoch, and then to Noah before and after the flood; also to Abraham quite often, to Abimelech in Genesis 20:3, to Isaac in Genesis 26:2, to Jacob in Genesis 28:13, to Laban in Genesis 31:24, to Moses in Exodus 3:2, and from then on very frequently speaking with him face to face, as a man with a man (Numbers 12:6, 7). He likewise allowed himself to be seen in Exodus 24:9, 10. To Joshua he appeared in the form of a man with a sword (Joshua 5:13). The visions of the Prophets are well known. If any is to be compared with that *Pimandrean* vision, it is that of Gabriel in Daniel 9:21, or chapter 10:5, or the Son of God who appeared to John on Patmos in almost similar form in Revelation 1:13, 14, where John says he was in the spirit and heard a voice behind him—which they interpret as an Ecstasy, just as Paul was caught up into heaven in the Temple at Jerusalem. So also we read of Peter in Acts 10:10 being freed by an angel. We can therefore concede such a "rapture" <term>Rapture (raptus): A state of being carried away by divine power or ecstasy, common in mystical literature.</term> even to Hermes, and it is likely he asked who it was that appeared to him. For Paul also asked in Acts 9:5, and so did Joshua. Truly, since Satan is the ape of God and transforms himself into an angel of light, it is necessary to discern the spirit. <margin>Revelations of God.</margin> *If it is of God, it speaks the words of God, nor does it command anything other than what is consistent with the Prophetic and Apostolic foundations.* If it brings forth anything else, it is not to be heard, even if it performs miracles (Deuteronomy 13). But it is most truly recognized if it proposes those things which God has forbidden, and which the pagan sages taught in Egypt, which was a diabolical doctrine and abominable to God. *Walk in my commandments*, says God; *walk not according to the ways of the nations* (Ezekiel 20:7, 11, 12, 18, 19, 20; Deuteronomy 4:1, 5, 6, etc.). And yet, the things which Poimandres proposes to his Hermes in the apparition are the dogmas of the Egyptian magi, which God forbade. Therefore, the apparition was by no means from the true God, but from a fraudulent demon. Is it from the light of nature, and Grace, or the Scripture that one seeks such doctrine? The glory of Paracelsus, Croll, Hartmann, and their like is to join both lights together. But they add Belial to Christ, and from idolatrous Egypt and the Magus they add to the light of nature—O, what fine Philosophers they are, who wish to be Christians! This is the first point. <margin>1 Peter 4:11.</margin>

**Secondly**: This Mercury <note>Hermes Trismegistus was identified with the Roman god Mercury.</note> says that *he has knowledge of the nature of things*. But this knowledge is not from an ecstatic rapture, but rather from the inspection of the world and practical experience through the ordinary mode, by which we recognize God's creatures (and especially ourselves) by the aid of the senses and reason, or we learn what is known and proven by others; and also from the study of creation and doctrine received from our ancestors by hand or by writing. Thus the Apostle Paul discusses this in Romans chapter 1, and así Paul the Hermit judged, and it is the same ordinary way still today. Why do even the Paracelsians commend the *Light of Nature*, *Chiromancy* <note>Palm reading.</note>, and *Chemical Anatomy* if nature is to be learned from a rapture?

**Thirdly**: Hermes says *he raised the sharpness of his mind to supernal things*; he ought first to have seen those things which wander before his feet in the present. But what "supernal things"? Those abstractions of the **Enthusiast** philosophers <note>Enthusiastae: Here meaning people who claim direct, private divine inspiration outside of scripture.</note> such as occur in the writings of Plato concerning the contemplation of *Ideas* and the "back of the super-heaven." But it is all too clear that those are false and erroneous specters. If you even say that things of eternity and the archetypes in God are meant, you have deceived yourself and others, since no one can look into God, and the things to be contemplated are subject to the senses. *Be not lifted up on high*, and do not be anxious about those things which exceed your grasp. For you shall not comprehend them (Luke 12:29, Romans 12:16, 11:33, 34). And God *made such wise men fools* (Isaiah 33:18, 1 Corinthians 1:20). Thus Plato also philosophized. But the Church of God does not acknowledge that Philosophy. <margin>Sirach 3:22. Seek not things higher than yourself. Proverbs 25:27. read also the following.</margin>

**Fourthly**: That *Genius* <note>A guiding spirit.</note> who appeared in Giant form indicates by his question that he is not God, nor the mind of God. For why was it necessary to ask what Mercury wanted to hear and see? The hearing and seeing of divine things does not depend on the human will, and it had already been told to man in the Church of God what he ought to hear and see. If he did not understand the desire of *Hermes*, it is plain he did not understand everything, and thus could not satisfy the desire any faster. *Divine wisdom is acquired after prayers have been sent ahead*; it is not offered idly to those sleeping or occupied in deep slumber. If we attribute anything to such apparitions, then we must believe the visions of Tundal, of the Emperor Louis, and of other fanatical and ecstatic magi or superstitious people. *He has dreamed, I have dreamed*, said the false prophets in Jeremiah 23:25. But what of God? *He calls it a lie*, and wants His own words set forth. Thus we would have had to believe the **Enthusiasts** or **Anabaptists** <note>A radical wing of the Reformation often accused of relying on private visions rather than scripture.</note> of former times, **John of Leiden**, and **Müntzer**. <margin>Isaiah 8:19. Deuteronomy 12:1, 2, 3.</margin>

<summary>The author argues that the Hermetic revelations used by Paracelsians like Croll and Hartmann are diabolical deceptions rather than divine truths. He asserts that true knowledge comes from Scripture and the "ordinary" study of nature, rather than the pagan ecstasies of Hermes Trismegistus.</summary>

<keywords>Paracelsianism, Oswald Croll, Hermes Trismegistus, Poimandres, Johannes Hartmann, Enthusiasm, Anabaptism, biblical revelation, alchemy vs scripture, false prophecy</keywords>

--- Page 106 ---
<meta>The previous page established the author's argument that the "magical union" sought by Paracelsians like Oswald Croll and Heinrich Khunrath is a dangerous delusion. This page continues that critique by dissecting the *Pimander*, a foundational Hermetic text, and contrasting its claims with Biblical doctrine to prove it is of demonic rather than divine origin.</meta>

### On the horrible impiety of Paracelsian Magic

...to **Müntzer** <note>Thomas Müntzer (c. 1489–1525), a radical Reformation theologian and leader of the German Peasants' War, often associated by his critics with dangerous mystical "revelations."</note> and his associates, who filled Germany with peasant turmoil. There is no old crone who does not value her own dreams highly, nor any witch or sorcerer who does not do the same.

<margin>Pimander or *Poimandres*.</margin>
Fifthly, when the guiding spirit <note>original: "genius"</note> was asked who he was, he called himself **Pimander**. Yet we read nowhere that this name was attributed to the true God or his angel. It sounds, however, the same as *Poimandres* or *Poimen andron* <note>original: "ποιμάνδρης ἢ ποιμὴν ἀνδρῶν"</note>, meaning "King" or "Shepherd of Men." It is not the nature of a King to profess the nature of physical things. Therefore, we understand that he has assigned himself a function that belongs to another and deserted his own. Physics must be learned from physicists, not from the shepherds of men or the presidents of republics. Our **Magi** <note>Referring here to the Paracelsian alchemists/occultists.</note>, therefore, while they admire that writing, show themselves to be demented by a vain superstition, and they do not consider what spirit it is they are following.

Sixthly, he lies when he says **he is the mind of divine power**. Although you might admit this is phrased inconsistently—because divine power and the mind of God are distinct properties of God—nevertheless, if he were that "mind," he would be the **Wisdom of God**. To this Wisdom is attributed whatever wise teaching exists in the first place, as in Proverbs 1, and chapters 8 and 9; Wisdom 6, 7, 8, and 9; and elsewhere. Psalm 104:24 says: *In Wisdom—that is, the Son—you have made all things* <note>original: "Dea [sic, likely 'Omnia'] in Sapientia... fecit"</note>, he who was made wisdom for us. See Colossians 1:16, John 1:3, and 1 Corinthians 1:30. **Hermes** <note>Hermes Trismegistus.</note> himself says: *The Word is the Image and mind of God. In him are hidden all the treasures of wisdom and knowledge* <note>Colossians 2:3.</note>. He himself gives men what they know (Psalm 94:10), who also knows that the thoughts of men (such as Hermes) are vain (ibid. verse 11). However, the Son of God cannot be understood as appearing in that vision [to Hermes]. The reason is: because although he has revealed to us the secrets of God pertaining to salvation, he indicated none of these things. *He said nothing of such a union or conjunction with God.* Furthermore, the things contained in the *Pimander* fight

<margin>Impious doctrines of the *Pimander*.</margin>
head-on against the revealed mysteries of the Church. At least look at these from Dialogue XII: "Most of all," says that devilish **Pimander**, "the man who grasps God and is conformed to the divine essence is immortal. For to him alone, out of every kind of living thing, God himself comes to meet: by night indeed through dreams, and by day more frequently through portents, through which he foretells all future things to himself BY BIRDS (Augury), ENTRAILS (Extispicy), a Spirit (a Python <note>A spirit of divination, named after the Delphic Oracle's serpent.</note>), and the Sibyl, because of which a man is truly said to know what things are past and future." Also: "Mind is God in men. Therefore, some from the number of men are gods, and their humanity is closest to divinity."

<margin>The Paracelsian life of all things, and Plato’s world.</margin>
They sin by an evil fate, and they suffer so that they may suffer; therefore, the shameful act is committed (which is the *faith of Predestinations*). The human soul is superior to fate. There is nothing in the world devoid of life. God willed the world to be a living thing. Whence it is necessary that the world be God. Truly, there is no need for more. The piously learned person immediately detects the devilish lies and the dogmas of ethnic <note>i.e., Pagan.</note> religion often forbidden by God.

Seventhly: **Hermes** desires to learn the nature of things and to know God. If **Pimander** had been the spirit of truth, he would have begun from the true creation of the World, and taught about the *integrity* and the *fall* of man. If he had described man as he is in sin, he certainly would not have made him a "third god" and a "great miracle," much less elevated him into God himself. The author of **Sirach** <note>original: "Syracides." Referring to the Wisdom of Ben Sira / Ecclesiasticus.</note> was also in Egypt. But for what he thinks of man, see chapter 10, verse 9 and following, where man is called earth, ashes, the slave of doctors, *the spoil of death, the food of worms*, serpents, and toads. What is man in God's judgment? Psalm 78:39: *He remembered that they are but flesh, a spirit that passes away and does not return.* See Psalm 90:7, Psalm 103:14–16, Psalm 39:5–6, Job 7:7, 10:9–10, 11:11, 13:28, 14 (entire), 15:16, 17:14, 20 (entire), 21:26, 25:6. Behold the "Great Miracle" of Man! Where are you, Hermes? Where are you, **Croll**, who repeated this from Hermes? The beginning of pride is to apostatize from God. Thus fell **Lucifer**, and the **first-formed parents** <note>original: "protoplasti," referring to Adam and Eve.</note>. **Pimander** ought to have taught these things and shown his Hermes the remedy from the revealed God, not from the "hidden God." But as the ancient serpent seduced Eve, so **Pimander** seduced his **Mercury** <note>Hermes is the Greek equivalent of the Roman Mercury.</note>: who, though he desires to know God, is drawn furthest away from God toward pagan auguries and the worship of demons.

Eighthly: **Pimander** commands that *he be grasped by the mind*. He had called himself the mind of divine power; he commands himself to be captured by the mind, so that "like may be captured by like." In Dialogue II, he explains the manner of capturing and understanding God: "Contemplate," he says, "God having all intelligences in himself. Unless you make yourself equal to God, you will never understand God. For like is always known by its like," etc. You see the fraudulent spirit. He persuades man of that which is impossible for him. Thus, that wretched **Pimander** certainly never arrived at the knowledge of God. And because eternal life consists in the knowledge of God and Christ (John 17), it must be concluded that this little man—whom **Croll** places before our Philosophers and even some of our Theologians—is a slave of eternal death, bound in chains of eternal darkness. How then, O Paracelsian Magi, does this **Gabalistic Magic** <note>A term used by critics to conflate Kabbalah and magical practices.</note> of Hermes please you?

<margin>**Croll** ought to have noted this, who thought that through his "sidereal spirit" true wisdom was attracted, and all wisdom, arts, and sciences from the all-knowing sky, etc. What need then for the spirit Pimander and other means?</margin>
Ninthly: the promise is vain. "I," he says, "will instruct you in all things you have desired." It is, I say, a lie. For God never decreed to manifest all things to man in this age, but only those things which he proposed in the book of nature (in which, nevertheless, the greatest things lie hidden) and in the scriptures pertaining to salvation. What should be said of the future? Isaiah 64:4. In 1 Corinthians 2:9, Paul takes away wisdom from the princes of this age, but grants something to the pious ministers of Christ because of the revelation of the spirit and the mind of Christ; but how little that is, he teaches in Romans 11 near the end, and in 1 Corinthians 13:9–10. Christ promises his disciples the Holy Spirit in John, but one who would not say anything else except what is consistent with Christ's doctrine. And not even Christ taught other things than what he had heard from the Father and had in his commands. **Pimander**, however, being a most liberal and profuse promiser, proves by this very fact that he is not a spirit from God.

Tenthly: This unhappy **Hermes** writes that all things (which he had desired concerning nature and God) were suddenly revealed to him; but he was miserably deceived. For he had not yet learned to know himself, and was plunged by his spirit into the pit of eternal damnation, as can be understood from the many impieties of **Pimander** and **Asclepius** <note>Another key Hermetic text.</note>. And so much for these things from the *Pimander*. He who has the *Amphitheater* of **Khunrath** <note>Heinrich Khunrath (c. 1560–1605), author of the *Amphitheatrum Sapientiae Eternae* (Amphitheater of Eternal Wisdom).</note>, will see in the sixth image...

<summary>The author systematically refutes the Hermetic "Pimander," arguing that its claims of human divinity and total knowledge are demonic lies that contradict Biblical teachings on man's fallen nature and the limitations of human wisdom.</summary>
<keywords>Pimander, Poimandres, Hermes Trismegistus, Paracelsus, Oswald Croll, Heinrich Khunrath, Alchemy, Hermeticism, Idolatry, Augury, Extispicy, Wisdom of God, Original Sin</keywords>

--- Page 107 ---
<meta>Continuing the author’s polemic against Oswald Croll and the Hermetic tradition. On the previous page, the author argued that the "Pimander" (the guiding spirit of the Hermetic texts) is a deceptive demon rather than a divine messenger. Here, the author examines Croll's "magical ladder" and the concept of "sacred silence," arguing that these are actually masks for demonic sorcery.</meta>

<header>...according to Croll's opinion.</header>

<margin>The Magic of Thrasybulus.</margin>
In this image, one can see a globe standing in the middle of a triangle and a square, displaying three spheres in its circumference. In the center of these is the "ladder of union," which encompasses *faith, meditation, knowledge, love, hope, prayer, conjunction, frequency, familiarity, and likeness.* No doubt Croll thought these qualities were to be observed in that Kabbalistic <note>original: "Gabalistica"</note> union with God. If his explanation corresponded with the Holy Scriptures, something of value might have been said. Into the first sphere, he categorized the degrees of knowledge, which are: <margin>The degrees of magical knowledge.</margin> *the object, the medium, the external senses, the internal sense, the common sense, phantasy, lower and upper judgment, Reason, intellect, and the mind.* If he thought that one could ascend all the way to the Archetype—Jehovah—in this manner, he did not think in accordance with the Gospel scripture, which shows that Christ is the *true door and the true ladder*, and the Spirit is the guide through the doctrine of faith. *No one comes to Christ unless it is given by the Father, and whom the Father draws.* (John 6, verse 44). It is a wonder that his memory failed him here.

In the preceding figure, upon which he inscribed the Ten Commandments in Hebrew along with the names of God and others, he ascends through the *En-Soph* <note>The "Ein Sof" or the Infinite in Kabbalistic thought.</note> to the magical triangle of Jehovah. The Magi also have fifty "gates of intelligence" derived from the five predicables and ten predicaments multiplied among themselves. In the second book of *Caelius Augustinus*, who added several things to the hieroglyphs of *Pierius*, there is an image of a Capricorn leaping out of a certain house. This is said to be called <margin>The magical Gate of the Gods.</margin> the "Gate of the Gods" by the Platonists, because they claim that souls, once released from the chains of the body, ascend to heaven through it, where they become participants in the divine nature. This too is a magical dream pertaining to the Kabbalistic union; the demon is even accustomed to carry off witches and mages to their festivities in the form of a goat. But it is to be feared that they are being led to the underworld by <margin>Whoever desires more, let him read the treatise of Jacob Heilbronner on the Magical and Kabbalistic Demonomania of Pistorius, etc.</margin> Mercury. So much for the Kabbalistic union of the *created spirit with the uncreated God*, of the finite spirit with the immense God, of a miserable little man—mere straw and stubble—with God the consuming fire, of magical fiction with the truth, and of Belial with Christ. Croll continues.

---

### S E C T I O N VIII.

<margin>The double Word <note>original: "Logos duplex"</note></margin>
**G**od is addressed by the internal spirit, not by the power of words, but in sacred silence without the opening of the mouth or the drawing of breath.) Even the Philosophers recognize a double <term>Word</term> <note>original: "λόγον"</note>. For there is the <term>inner</term> <note>original: "ἔσω"</note> Word, which is also called <term>internal</term> <note>original: "ἐνδιάθετος"</note>, and the <term>outer</term> <note>original: "ἔξω"</note> Word, which is <term>uttered</term> <note>original: "προφορικός"</note>, which occurs when the formal idea conceived in the mind is committed to material sound, flows into the air, and is there multiplied due to the air's divisibility until it is carried to and perceived by the ears of those present. It is not necessary to let out shouts to God for His own sake; He understands even thoughts <term>as well as the inner word</term> <note>original: "καὶ τὸν ἔσω λόγον"</note>, as the example of Moses teaches in Exodus 14, verse 15. Yet the affections of the soul and the circumstances do indeed express a voice. It is well known how much the Magi attribute to words, characters, and other sensible signs. Not infrequently, they move their demons with other signs from which the demons easily understand what they want, <margin>See page 44 of Croll.</margin> although they uniquely crave the glory of knowing hearts.

That is indeed impious and diabolical: but *Croll*, along with other Kabbalists, dares to turn this into sacred prayers and "sacred silence," *as if, when the Magi seem to produce wonders, it happens through the power of silent prayers to God.* This is a part of Pythagorean Magic which they call <term>vow of silence</term> <note>original: "ἐχεμυθίαν"</note>. It is the opinion of the Chaldeans, <margin>The foundation of the devilish magic of the "Fork-ride" <note>A German pun on Kabbalah ("Gabal-") and the witch's fork ("Gabel").</note>, that is, the Kabbalistic ride.</margin> as Agrippa writes, *that a mind fixed with its whole focus on God can be filled with the divine power; and thus filled with light, it pours out rays through every medium even to this gross body, surrounding the body itself with copious light, making it like the stars, lifting it high with lightness, and sometimes transferring it across a long distance; others pass through closed doors:* they claim God must be invoked through mediators, souls, intelligences, and demons; and that clear prayer is useless, etc. *Croll* on page 34 writes: *The mind elevated to God the Most High and united with Him is preserved with God, and works by that same power; nor is anything found in man—no disposition (even sin? and the evils arising from it?)—in which something of divinity does not shine, nor is there anything in God that is not itself represented in man.* On page 71: *If the soul runs back into itself and turns toward the mind, it approaches God, sees all things, and has no need for external discipline, after the manner of angels who learn all things from within, etc.*

This is that precept of the *Pimander*, in which he commands Hermes to embrace himself with his own mind and receive information on everything he desires. The mind must be elevated through the fifty gates of intelligence on the Magical ladder into the *En-soph*, and from there, in turn, power must be brought down, as it pleases other Magi. All this is done not by oral but by mental invocation. Thus *Simon Magus* the Samaritan performed miracles by keeping silent. Thus *Trithemius*, and *Laurentius* (the tutor of Pope Hildebrand, who as Cardinal Benno writes was a master in the Magical art), *Michael Sicidites* of Constantinople (according to Nicetas Choniates), *Zython* the Bohemian (as *Dubravius* relates), *Diodorus Siculus* of Catania (according to Fazello), *Pafetes* the Magus, *Zedechias* the Jew (who lived in the time of Louis the Pious), *Eon* the heretic, the *Hostess of Apuleius*, *Apollonius of Tyana*, and countless other Magi procured wonders, although not always without a voice. Therefore, he who wishes to know what that *sacred silence of Croll* is, learns from this that it is a diabolical pretext of an abominable and cursed magic, so that simple people might be persuaded that what the devil of the magicians executes is actually done by the power of interior prayers. Such silence is execrable, not sacred. God, in being invoked, <margin>Croll's sacred silence. 1 Corinthians 4.</margin> also requires express words for the edification of others, as is done in the Church with the priest leading; and for the brightness of His glory even among the angels, and for the terror and flight of devils. The *Paracelsian* Kabbalist will perhaps deny that his deeds, done through silent prayers, are similar to those of these diabolical magicians; indeed, he will say his silence is sacred by its essence, while theirs is by imitation. For the devil is accustomed to imitate the deeds of God and the Saints. Truly, the histories of true miracles performed by the Prophets, Christ, and the Apostles do not mention this "sacred silence," but indicate that for the most part, words or external gestures were added. Even the Magi themselves confess that God created all things through the word "Let there be." Although God's speaking differs from the speech of men, it is nevertheless an argument for performing miracles by means of speech. Who would allow himself to be persuaded that a silence is sacred whose foundations and effects are the same [as magic]?

The author lists various historical and legendary magicians to demonstrate that these "silent" wonders are actually the work of the devil.</summary>
